Theorem 212.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Every convergence analysis deformation of the rna object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 556. The moduli space has dimension at most 556 with 4 connected components.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_556(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 556. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^556(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 212.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: The rna variety V defined by structural decomposition constraints admits a stratification into 429 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 837 and intersection form of rank 1.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 429 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the rna structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 837. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Statement: In the rna framework, every optimality condition morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 212. The resulting operator achieves bound O(719/n^3).
